Map RAGEN mode to AlfWorld train_eval\\n        self.current_mode = mode\\n        self._alfworld_mode = self._get_alfworld_mode(mode)\\n\\n        # Initialize raw environment\\n        self.raw_env = self._get_cached_raw_env(self._alfworld_mode)\\n        self.num_games = self.raw_env.num_games\\n        self.game_files = list(self.raw_env.game_files)\\n\\n        self.alfred_env = None\\n        self.current_game_file = None\\n        self.render_cache = None\\n        self.available_actions = None\\n        self.valid_action_count = 0\\n        self.render_mode = self.config.render_mode\\n        assert self.render_mode == 'text'\\n\\n    def _get_alfworld_mode(self, mode: str) -> str:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Convert RAGEN mode to AlfWorld train_eval string.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        if mode == 'train':\\n            return 'train'\\n        else:\\n            # Use eval_dataset from config for val/test modes\\n            return self.config.eval_dataset\\n\\n    def _get_cached_raw_env(self, alfworld_mode: str) -> AlfredTWEnv:\\n        cache_key = (os.path.abspath(self.config.config_file), alfworld_mode)\\n        raw_env = _RAW_ENV_CACHE.get(cache_key)\\n        if raw_env is None:\\n            raw_env = AlfredTWEnv(config=self.raw_env_config, train_eval=alfworld_mode)\\n            _RAW_ENV_CACHE[cache_key] = raw_env\\n        return raw_env\\n    \\n    def _reinitialize_for_mode(self, mode: str):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Reinitialize the environment when mode changes.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        new_alfworld_mode = self._get_alfworld_mode(mode)\\n\\n        if new_alfworld_mode != self._alfworld_mode:\\n            # Track valid actions for metrics\\n        if action_is_available:\\n            self.valid_action_count += 1\\n\\n        # Reward computation\\n        reward = self.config.score * float(won)\\n        if self.config.valid_action_bonus > 0:\\n            if self.config.success_gated_bonus:\\n                # Success-gated: bonus only awarded on successful episode completion\\n                if done and won:\\n                    reward += self.valid_action_count * self.config.valid_action_bonus\\n            else:\\n                # Unconditional: bonus per valid action step\\n                if action_is_available:\\n                    reward += self.config.valid_action_bonus\\n\\n        # Format observation with admissible actions\\n        self.render_cache = self._format_observation(observation, self.available_actions)\\n\\n        info = {\\n            \\\"action_is_effective\\\": True,\\n            \\\"action_is_valid\\\": action_is_available,\\n            \\\"success\\\": won\\n        }\\n\\n        return self.render_cache, reward, done, info\\n    \\n    def render(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Return current observation with admissible actions.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        return self.render_cache\\n\\n    def close(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Clean up environment resources.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        self.render_cache = None\\n        if hasattr(self, 'alfred_env') and self.alfred_env is not None:\\n            try:\\n                self.alfred_env.close()\\n            except Exception:\\n                pass\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    import os\\n    os.environ[\\\"ALFWORLD_DATA\\\"] = os.path.expanduser(\\\"~/.cache/alfworld\\\")\\n\\n    # Test step with admissible action\\n    print(\\\"\\\\n=== Test 3: Taking a step ===\\\")\\n    if env.available_actions:\\n        action = env.available_actions[0]\\n        print(f\\\"Taking action: {action}\\\")\\n        obs, reward, done, info = env.step(action)\\n        print(f\\\"Reward: {reward}, Done: {done}, Info: {info}\\\")\\n        print(f\\\"New observation (first 300 chars):\\\\n{obs[:300]}...\\\")\\n\\n    env.close()\\n    print(\\\"\\\\n=== All tests completed ===\\\")\\n    \\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 1.19}}"}

Map RAGEN mode to AlfWorld train_eval\\n        self.current_mode = mode\\n        self._alfworld_mode = self._get_alfworld_mode(mode)\\n\\n        # Initialize raw environment\\n        self.raw_env = self._get_cached_raw_env(self._alfworld_mode)\\n        self.num_games = self.raw_env.num_games\\n        self.game_files = list(self.raw_env.game_files)\\n\\n        self.alfred_env = None\\n        self.current_game_file = None\\n        self.render_cache = None\\n        self.available_actions = None\\n        self.valid_action_count = 0\\n        self.render_mode = self.config.render_mode\\n        assert self.render_mode == 'text'\\n\\n    def _get_alfworld_mode(self, mode: str) -> str:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Convert RAGEN mode to AlfWorld train_eval string.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        if mode == 'train':\\n            return 'train'\\n        else:\\n            # Use eval_dataset from config for val/test modes\\n            return self.config.eval_dataset\\n\\n    def _get_cached_raw_env(self, alfworld_mode: str) -> AlfredTWEnv:\\n        cache_key = (os.path.abspath(self.config.config_file), alfworld_mode)\\n        raw_env = _RAW_ENV_CACHE.get(cache_key)\\n        if raw_env is None:\\n            raw_env = AlfredTWEnv(config=self.raw_env_config, train_eval=alfworld_mode)\\n            _RAW_ENV_CACHE[cache_key] = raw_env\\n        return raw_env\\n    \\n    def _reinitialize_for_mode(self, mode: str):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Reinitialize the environment when mode changes.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        new_alfworld_mode = self._get_alfworld_mode(mode)\\n\\n        if new_alfworld_mode != self._alfworld_mode:\\n            # Key findings so far:

1. **AlfredEnvConfig defaults**: `score: float = 10.0` — so by default, reward = 10.0 * won. This is correct.

2. **AlfworldSG tag in envs.yaml**: Only sets `eval_dataset: "eval_in_distribution"` in `env_config`. Does NOT set `score`, so it would use the default 10.0.

3. **exp016 config**: Uses tag `AlfworldSG`, doesn't override `score`. So the env should have `score=10.0`.
   
I can't find es_manager.py at the expected path, and exp013f doesn't exist — only exp013 and exp013b are available. The reward calculation in env.py looks right: `reward = self.config.score * float(won)` with `score=10.0` by default should give 10.0 when the task succeeds. The issue must be downstream — either the reward gets lost somewhere in the es_manager or GRPO pipeline, or the `won` flag isn't actually being set to True during execution.

I need to locate es_manager.py and trace how rewards flow through the system, plus check how `format_penalty` in the config might be interfering.

what seed is used to reset this environment\\n\\n\\n\\nclass EnvStateManager:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Manager for the environment state\\n    The class is responsible for managing multiple (kinds of) environments\\n    \\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    def __init__(self, config, mode: str = \\\"train\\\"):\\n        self.sys_config = config\\n        self.mode = mode\\n        self.config = getattr(self.sys_config.es_manager, mode)\\n        self.env_groups = int(self.config.env_groups)\\n        self.group_size = self.config.group_size\\n        seed_cfg = getattr(self.sys_config, \\\"seed\\\", None)\\n        if seed_cfg is not None:\\n            self.base_seed = seed_cfg.get(mode, None)\\n        else:\\n            self.base_seed = None\\n        self.seed_counter = 0\\n        self._init_envs()\\n        self.rollout_cache = None\\n        self._executors: Dict[str, ThreadPoolExecutor] = {}\\n        self._executors_shutdown = False\\n        self._register_parallel_executors()\\n        atexit.register(self._shutdown_executors)\\n\\n    def _init_envs(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Initialize the environments. train_envs and val_envs are lists of envs:\\n        Input: tags: [\\\"SimpleSokoban\\\", \\\"HarderSokoban\\\"]; n_groups: [1, 1]; group_size: 16\\n        Output: envs: List[Dict], each **entry** is a dict with keys: tag, group_id, env_id, env, env_config, status\\n        Example: [{\\\"tag\\\": \\\"SimpleSokoban\\\", \\\"group_id\\\": 0, \\\"env_id\\\": 0, \\\"env\\\": env, \\\"config\\\": env_config, \\\"status\\\": EnvStatus()},\\n            ...\\n            {\\\"tag\\\": \\\"SimpleSokoban\\\", \\\"group_id\\\": 0, \\\"env_id\\\": 15 (group_size - 1), ...},\\n            {\\\"tag\\\": \\\"HarderSokoban\\\", \\\"group_id\\\": 1, \\\"env_id\\\": 16, ...}\\n            ...]\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        assert sum(self.config.env_configs.n_groups) == self.env_groups, f\\\"Sum of n_groups must equal env_groups. what seed is used to reset this environment\\n\\n\\n\\nclass EnvStateManager:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Manager for the environment state\\n    The class is responsible for managing multiple (kinds of) environments\\n    \\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    def __init__(self, config, mode: str = \\\"train\\\"):\\n        self.sys_config = config\\n        self.mode = mode\\n        self.config = getattr(self.sys_config.es_manager, mode)\\n        self.env_groups = int(self.config.env_groups)\\n        self.group_size = self.config.group_size\\n        seed_cfg = getattr(self.sys_config, \\\"seed\\\", None)\\n        if seed_cfg is not None:\\n            self.base_seed = seed_cfg.get(mode, None)\\n        else:\\n            self.base_seed = None\\n        self.seed_counter = 0\\n        self._init_envs()\\n        self.rollout_cache = None\\n        self._executors: Dict[str, ThreadPoolExecutor] = {}\\n        self._executors_shutdown = False\\n        self._register_parallel_executors()\\n        atexit.register(self._shutdown_executors)\\n\\n    def _init_envs(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Initialize the environments. train_envs and val_envs are lists of envs:\\n        Input: tags: [\\\"SimpleSokoban\\\", \\\"HarderSokoban\\\"]; n_groups: [1, 1]; group_size: 16\\n        Output: envs: List[Dict], each **entry** is a dict with keys: tag, group_id, env_id, env, env_config, status\\n        Example: [{\\\"tag\\\": \\\"SimpleSokoban\\\", \\\"group_id\\\": 0, \\\"env_id\\\": 0, \\\"env\\\": env, \\\"config\\\": env_config, \\\"status\\\": EnvStatus()},\\n            ...\\n            {\\\"tag\\\": \\\"SimpleSokoban\\\", \\\"group_id\\\": 0, \\\"env_id\\\": 15 (group_size - 1), ...},\\n            {\\\"tag\\\": \\\"HarderSokoban\\\", \\\"group_id\\\": 1, \\\"env_id\\\": 16, ...}\\n            ...]\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        assert sum(self.config.env_configs.n_groups) == self.env_groups, f\\\"Sum of n_groups must equal env_groups. status.num_actions\\n            if turn_done:\\n                status.terminated = True # TODO check terminated definition in gymnasium\\n                status.truncated = not turn_info.get('success', False)\\n            history = self._update_cache_history(history, next_state=obs, actions_left=actions_left, num_actions_info={\\n                'actions': executed_actions, 'reward': acc_reward, 'info': turn_info,\\n                'llm_response': env_input['llm_response'], 'llm_raw_response': env_input['llm_raw_response']\\n            })\\n            # filter out invalid actions\\n            # history = [content for content in history[:-1] if content['actions']] + [history[-1]]\\n            return status, history\\n\\n        envs = self.envs\\n        env_outputs = []\\n\\n        def _process_env_input(env_input: Dict) -> Dict:\\n            acc_reward, turn_info, turn_done = 0, {}, False\\n            entry = envs[env_input['env_id']]\\n            env_id, env = entry['env_id'], entry['env']\\n            actions_left_before = entry['max_actions_per_traj'] - entry['status'].num_actions\\n\\n            # execute actions in envs\\n            valid_actions = self._extract_map_valid_actions(entry, env_input['actions'])\\n            acc_reward, turn_info, turn_done, executed_actions = _execute_actions(env, valid_actions[:actions_left_before])\\n            no_manager_action = len(valid_actions) == 0\\n            penalty_delta = 0.0\\n            if len(valid_actions) != len(env_input['actions']) or not valid_actions:\\n                penalty_delta = self.sys_config.es_manager.format_penalty\\n            if no_manager_action:\\n                turn_info = dict(turn_info)\\n                turn_info['manager_invalid_action'] = True\\n\\n            status, history = _log_env_state(entry['status'], self.rollout_cache[env_id]['history'], entry['env'].render(), entry['max_actions_per_traj'], executed_actions, valid_actions, acc_reward, turn_done, turn_info, env_input)\\n            if no_manager_action and history:\\n                history[-1]['manager_invalid_action'] = True\\n            if status.num_actions >= entry['max_actions_per_traj'] and not turn_done:\\n                status.truncated = True\\n                status.terminated = True\\n                turn_done = True\\n\\n            return {\\n                'env_id': env_id,\\n                'status': status,\\n                'history': history,\\n                'turn_done': turn_done,\\n                'penalty_delta': penalty_delta,\\n            }\\n\\n        results: List[Optional[Dict]] = [None] * len(all_env_inputs)\\n        tag2items: Dict[str, List[tuple]] = {}\\n        for idx, env_input in enumerate(all_env_inputs):\\n            entry = envs[env_input['env_id']]\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.41}}"}

````text
Now I have a good picture of the es_manager.py flow. Let me trace the reward path:

1. In `_execute_actions`: calls `env.step(action)` → gets `reward`. Accumulates `acc_reward += reward`. Also tries to get `raw_acc_reward += float(info.get('raw_reward', 0.0))`.

2. But wait - the env.py's `step()` returns `info` as:
```python
info = {
    "action_is_effective": True,
    "action_is_valid": action_is_available,
    "success": won
}
```
There's NO `raw_reward` field in the info dict! So `info.get('raw_reward', 0.0)` always returns 0.0.

3. The `acc_reward` from `_execute_actions` should be the actual reward from env.step().

4. In `_log_env_state`, `status.rewards.append(acc_reward)` - so rewards are stored per turn.

5. Later in `get_rollout_states()`, there's a check for `raw_reward` in custom metrics that calculates episodic return, but since that field never gets populated from the env, this calculation would always be zero.

The real question is where the actual accumulated rewards from `status.rewards` get used in the GRPO training loop to compute advantages and update the policy. Looking at how `get_rollout_states()` processes the turn history, it collects all `raw_reward` values from the info dictionaries into a custom metrics list. Since the environment is setting `raw_reward` to 0 in each turn's info, this creates a list of zeros that gets summed up and logged as `episodic_return` = 0 in the metrics.

These metrics are just for logging though — the actual rewards used for GRPO training should come from `status.rewards`, which gets the correct values from `env.step()`. So I need to check whether the GRPO advantage computation is actually pulling from `status.rewards` or if it's somehow using the `raw_reward` metrics instead.

If advantages and returns are also showing as 0 in the logs, that suggests the training signal itself is broken, not just the logging. Let me trace through the code to see how rewards flow from the rollout into the actual training loop.

### Assistant

_source line 99_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
Now I see the key parts. For the single-turn mode (lines ~957-1040):

```python
# Collect episode-level rewards for normalization
episode_raw_rewards = []
episode_penalties = []
for env_output in env_outputs:
    history = self._extract_history(env_output, prepare_for_update=True)
    total_reward = sum(turn.get('reward', 0.0) for turn in history)
    episode_raw_rewards.append(total_reward)
    episode_penalties.append(env_output.get("penalty", 0))


```

Then I'm normalizing those collected rewards and building a score tensor where each sample gets its normalized reward assigned to the final position. The flow aggregates all turn rewards from each environment's history, applies normalization accounting for penalties, and maps those scores into a tensor for training. So the reward flow is: each turn accumulates rewards from `env.step()`, which returns 10.0 only when the episode succeeds, then all turn rewards get summed into `episode_raw_rewards`. But I'm wondering if `_extract_history` might be truncating the history in a way that drops the final turn where the reward was actually awarded — in AlfWorld's multi-turn setup, the agent takes many steps with zero reward until the last one succeeds and grants the full 10.0 points.
````

Now I have a clear picture. Let me also look at what context window mode exp016 uses. The key is:

1. `_extract_history` with `prepare_for_update=True` removes the trailing state-only entry (no llm_response).
2. `total_reward = sum(turn.get('reward', 0.0) for turn in history)` sums rewards from all turns.

The env.step() correctly returns reward=10.0 when won=True. And `_execute_actions` accumulates `acc_reward += reward`. And `_log_env_state` stores `status.rewards.append(acc_reward)`.


Each history entry contains the state, actions, accumulated reward, info, and the LLM response, so the winning turn should have reward=10.0 stored in its history entry. Now I need to trace through the `_normalize_episode_rewards` function to see how it handles grouping and reward normalization...

The function only normalizes rewards when there are fewer groups than total outputs, meaning if each output belongs to a unique group, no normalization happens. With GRPO, grouping is based on the initial state, so rewards within each group get normalized using the specified method. For "mean_std" normalization, there's a critical issue: if all samples in a group have identical rewards, the standard deviation becomes zero, which causes the normalization to fail.

Actually, this might be the intended behavior—when all samples have the same reward, there's no gradient signal to learn from. Looking at exp016 specifically, with 4 groups of 16 samples each and only about 3 successes out of 64 total, most groups will have either zero or one success, meaning nearly all rewards in those groups are identical and get zeroed out during normalization.

The real issue seems to be that even though the environment reports 4.7% success at step 7, the reward signal isn't being propagated properly—either the validation success rate differs from the training rate, or there's a disconnect between when the environment marks episodes as won and when those rewards actually get computed. So I'm tracing where `raw_reward` comes from in the logging. The manager's rollout function aggregates `raw_reward` from a custom metrics dictionary, which accumulates `raw_acc_reward` values collected during each step from the environment's info dict. But when I look at the actual environment code, the info dictionary doesn't include a `raw_reward` field at all—it only has `action_is_effective` and `action_is_valid`. That's the disconnect: the code is trying to log a reward signal that the environment never actually provides.

This is just a logging artifact though. The real training reward comes from `acc_reward` in `_execute_actions`, which properly pulls from `env.step()`. But if the actual reward signal is non-zero, then advantages and returns should reflect that. Let me check what's actually being logged for those values to understand where the zero signal is really coming from.

I need to trace through which context mode exp016 is using for building the update DataProto — there are multiple paths depending on the `max_context_window` configuration, and I should verify which one is active and what reward normalization settings are in play.

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"    def _build_samples_full(\\n        self, env_outputs: List[Dict], include_collapse_data: bool = True\\n    ) -> DataProto:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Build full multi-turn samples for update (original behavior).\\n        All assistant turns are trained.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        llm_input_texts = []\\n        messages_list = []\\n        collapse_cfg = getattr(self.config, \\\"collapse_detection\\\", None)\\n        first_turn_enabled = bool(getattr(collapse_cfg, \\\"first_turn_enabled\\\", False)) if collapse_cfg else False\\n        multi_turn_enabled = bool(getattr(collapse_cfg, \\\"multi_turn_enabled\\\", False)) if collapse_cfg else False\\n        collapse_enabled = first_turn_enabled or multi_turn_enabled\\n\\n        include_collapse_data = include_collapse_data and collapse_enabled\\n        include_first_turn = include_collapse_data and first_turn_enabled\\n        include_all_turns = include_collapse_data and multi_turn_enabled\\n\\n        first_turn_prompt_ids_list = [] if include_first_turn else None\\n        first_turn_reasoning_ids_list = [] if include_first_turn else None\\n        # Multi-turn data for collapse detection\\n        all_turns_prompt_ids_list = [] if include_all_turns else None\\n        all_turns_reasoning_ids_list = [] if include_all_turns else None\\n        turn_counts_list = [] if include_all_turns else None\\n        turn_counts_total_list = [] if include_all_turns else None\\n\\n        for env_output in env_outputs:\\n            history = self._extract_history(env_output, prepare_for_update=True)\\n            if include_first_turn:\\n                first_prompt_ids, first_reasoning_ids = self._build_first_turn_prompt_and_reasoning_ids(\\n                    env_output, history\\n                )\\n                first_turn_prompt_ids_list.append(first_prompt_ids)\\n                first_turn_reasoning_ids_list.append(first_reasoning_ids)\\n\\n            # Build multi-turn prompt/reasoning for collapse detection\\n            if include_all_turns:\\n                all_prompt_ids, all_reasoning_ids = self._build_all_turns_prompt_and_reasoning_ids(\\n                    env_output, history\\n                )\\n                all_turns_prompt_ids_list.append(all_prompt_ids)\\n                all_turns_reasoning_ids_list.append(all_reasoning_ids)\\n                turn_counts_list.append(len(all_prompt_ids))\\n                total_turns = sum(1 for turn in history if \\\"llm_response\\\" in turn)\\n                turn_counts_total_list.append(total_turns)\\n            messages = [\\n                {\\\"role\\\": \\\"system\\\", \\\"content\\\": self._build_system_content(env_output[\\\"env_id\\\"])},\\n                {\\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\", \\\"content\\\": \\\"\\\"}\\n            ]\\n\\n            for idx, content in enumerate(history):\\n                actual_turn = idx + 1\\n                if \\\"state\\\" in content:\\n                    messages[-1][\\\"content\\\"] += self._build_turn_state_content(\\n                        content, actual_turn, env_output[\\\"env_id\\\"]\\n                    )\\n                if \\\"llm_response\\\" in content:\\n                    messages.append({\\\"role\\\": \\\"assistant\\\", \\\"content\\\": content[\\\"llm_response\\\"]})\\n                if \\\"reward\\\" in content and idx < len(history) - 1:\\n                    messages.append({\\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\", \\\"content\\\": f\\\"Reward:\\\\n{content['reward']}\\\\n\\\"})\\n\\n            # Apply max length truncation\\n            messages = self._apply_max_length(messages, add_generation_prompt=False)\\n\\n            text = self.tokenizer.apply_chat_template(messages, add_generation_prompt=False, tokenize=False)\\n            llm_input_texts.append(text)\\n            messages_list.append(messages)\\n\\n        # Tokenize\\n        input_ids, attention_mask, position_ids = self._tokenize_and_build_tensors(llm_input_texts)\\n\\n        # Build scores using existing logic\\n        scores = [[i.get('reward', 0.0) for i in self._extract_history(env_output, True)] for env_output in env_outputs]\\n        score_tensor, loss_mask, response_mask = get_masks_and_scores(\\n            input_ids, self.tokenizer, scores,\\n            use_turn_scores=self.config.agent_pro\\n\\n...     input_ids, self.tokenizer, scores,
    use_turn_scores=self.config.agent_proxy.use_turn_scores,
    ...
)
```

`use_turn_scores = False` (from base config, not overridden in exp016).


When that flag is off, the code sums all turn rewards and assigns the total to the final token position, so an episode with a 10.0 reward on success would accumulate to that single value at the end. I'm noticing something around line 1212 that seems cut off—let me check what's happening there.
````

</details>

# Build scores using existing logic
scores = [[i.get('reward', 0.0) for i in self._extract_history(env_output, True)] for env_output in env_outputs]
score_tensor, loss_mask, response_mask = get_masks_and_scores(
    input_ids, self.tokenizer, scores,
    use_turn_scores=self.config.agent_proxy.use_turn_scores,
    enable_response_mask=self.config.enable_response_mask
)

# Normalize scores
if not self.config.agent_proxy.use_turn_scores:
    score_tensor = self._normalize_score_tensor(score_tensor, env_outputs)

````text
Now I have the full picture. Let me trace the flow:

1. `env.py` step() returns `reward = self.config.score * float(won)` = 10.0 when won=True
2. `es_manager.py` `_execute_actions` accumulates `acc_reward += reward`
3. The reward is stored in history entries: `history[-1]['reward'] = acc_reward`
4. In `_build_samples_full()`:
   ```python
   scores = [[i.get('reward', 0.0) for i in self._extract_history(env_output, True)] for env_output in env_outputs]
   ```
   This extracts per-turn rewards from history.
5. `get_masks_and_scores()` with `use_turn_scores=False` sums all turn rewards and places the total at the last token position.
6. `_normalize_score_tensor()` with `method="identity"` and `grouping="state"` applies no transformation to the scores.

So the reward should flow correctly through the pipeline. Let me check if there's an issue with how the scores are being computed or applied.

Actually, wait — I need to understand what "raw_reward" means in the WandB logs. Looking at the code, `raw_reward` gets populated from the turn info dictionary, where it collects all non-success metrics from the history and sums them to compute the episodic return.

Tracing the metric collection...

The issue is clear now: the environment's info dict never includes a `raw_reward` field — it only has `action_is_effective`, `action_is_valid`, and `success`. So when the code accumulates `raw_acc_reward` by pulling from `info.get('raw_reward', 0.0)`, it's always getting the default 0.0, which means `raw_reward` in the logs is perpetually zero. This is a bug in the metric collection pipeline.

The actual training signal comes through a different path: `history['reward']` pulls from `acc_reward` in `_execute_actions`, which is the legitimate return value from `env.step()`. So the model is training on the right reward, but the logged metrics are misleading. Now I need to trace through how `advantages` and `returns` are computed in the GRPO algorithm to see if there are similar issues there.
````

Now I see that `compute_reward` calls `self.reward_fn(data, return_dict=True)`. The `reward_fn` is an `AbstractRewardManager` that is loaded using `load_reward_manager`. This is the reward manager that computes the training reward.

The key question is: what reward manager does RAGEN use, and how does it compute the reward? 

Let me look at the `NaiveRewardManager` (since `reward_manager_name` defaults to "naive") and what it does with the batch. Specifically, it probably reads `rm_scores` from the batch (which was set from env rewards by the ctx_manager).


So there's a distinction here: `rm_scores` gets populated in the batch from environment rewards during rollout formulation, but then the reward manager actually recomputes the training reward from the batch data rather than using those scores directly. The computed reward tensor becomes the token-level scores and rewards for training.

The issue is that `NaiveRewardManager` might be using its default `compute_score` function, which is designed for text-based tasks comparing against ground truth. But in the RAGEN multi-turn setting, the reward is already coming from the environment and stored in `rm_scores` — the reward manager might not know how to extract those environment rewards and could default to 0 instead. I need to check how the reward manager gets initialized in the agent trainer to understand what's actually happening.
